      = false
      cite: [f1099div] p. 5, Instructions for Recipient, Box 13, "Shows exempt-interest dividends subject to the alternative minimum tax" (January 2024 (continuous-use form); 4ea1de80)
  (helper) Unsupported section 897, liquidation or unreconciled basis/excess distribution
      = false
      cite: [f1099div] p. 5, Instructions for Recipient, boxes 2e, 2f, 3, 9 and 10, "Box 2e. Shows the portion of the amount in box 1a that is section 897 gain", "Box 2f. Shows the portion of the amount in box 2a that is section 897 gain", "Box 3. Shows a return of capital. To the extent of your cost (or other basis) in the stock, the distribution reduces your basis and is not taxable", "Boxes 9 and 10. Show cash and noncash liquidation distributions" (January 2024 (continuous-use form); 4ea1de80)
  (helper) A gain component exceeds total capital gain distributions
      = false
      cite: [f1099div] p. 5, Instructions for Recipient, boxes 2b to 2d, "Box 2b. Shows the portion of the amount in box 2a that is unrecaptured section 1250 gain", "Box 2c. Shows the portion of the amount in box 2a that is section 1202 gain", "Box 2d. Shows the portion of the amount in box 2a that is 28% rate gain" (January 2024 (continuous-use form); 4ea1de80)
      note: Interpretation amb.2025.div_gain_portions_disjoint: The first (div.bad_special tests div.special_total, boxes 2b plus 2c plus 2d, against box 2a). The issuer instructions define each box by a different kind of property: box 2b is unrecaptured section 1250 gain "from certain depreciable real property", box 2c section 1202 gain "from certain qualified small business stock", box 2d 28% rate gain "from sales or exchanges of collectibles"; and box 2a must "Include all amounts shown in boxes 2b, 2c, 2d, and 2f". No one gain comes from two of those kinds. Box 2f (section 897 gain from US real property interests) can share an amount with box 2b, and it is not in the sum. The reading can only refuse an entry, never change a computed amount, and any box 2c amount is refused in any case (div.section1202). Recorded under T-006.
 (i1099div, p. 4, Box 2a, "Include all amounts shown in boxes 2b, 2c, 2d, and 2f"; Box 2b; Box 2c; Box 2d)
  (helper) Zero only after distribution scope checks
      = $0
      cite: [f1099div] Instructions for Recipient, boxes 1–16 (January 2024 (continuous-use form); 4ea1de80)
      note: Interpretation amb.2025.box2c_scope: Refuse nonzero box 2c; no exclusion or AMT amount is silently omitted. Archived Schedule D instructions require the exclusion on Form 8949 code Q and describe an AMT adjustment for 50/60/75-percent exclusions. The existing explicit exclusions are retained; the owner was notified and asked, then said continue without selecting an expansion. (i1040sd, p. 8, Gain from Form 1099-DIV and Alternative Minimum Tax)

== Form 4952 — supported investment-interest deduction and election ==
  (helper) Did you or your spouse pay or accrue any interest in 2025 on money borrowed for investments (even if the interest was for an earlier period), or does either of you have investment interest expense carried forward from an earlier year? Answer Yes if any of these applies:

      - interest on any loan of which any part was used to buy or hold property held for investment, such as stocks, bonds, funds, land held for appreciation, royalty interests, collectibles, cryptocurrency, a loan you made to someone else, an oil or gas working interest you do not materially participate in, or any other property held for investment; this includes brokerage margin interest, and a home equity loan, personal or family loan or credit card used that way, even if you received a Form 1098 for it
      - interest on a loan used to buy an interest in a partnership or S corporation
      - investment interest expense reported to you on a Schedule K-1 from a partnership or an S corporation

      = $0

      = $0
      cite: [f4137] p. 1, line 4, "Subtract line 3 from line 2. Include as income on Form 1040, 1040-SR, or 1040-NR, line 1c" (2025; created 2025-03-27 (the revision for tax year 2025, archived from irs-prior after irs.gov posted a later revision); f2f7a822)
      note: Interpretation amb.2025.f4137_reported_over_received: The first (f4137.line4 subtracts with a floor of zero). Line 4 is "Include as income on Form 1040 ... line 1c", the tips not reported; a negative unreported amount has no meaning there, and column (c) is to "Include all cash and charge tips you received", so it holds at least the tips reported. The reading matters only for inconsistent entries. (f4137, p. 1, line 4, "Subtract line 3 from line 2. Include as income on Form 1040, 1040-SR, or 1040-NR, line 1c"; p. 2, Columns (c) and (d), "Include all cash and charge tips you received")

      = false
      cite: [i172] p. 2, "Only NOLs arising after 2017 and carried forward to a year after 2020 are subject to the 80%-of-taxable-income limit" (Rev. December 2024 (Instructions for Form 172; the current irs-pdf edition; names 2018 through 2025); 15977bb6)
      note: taken: {"eq":["scope.nol",{"const":false}],"op":"test"}; Interpretation amb.2025.s1_nol_limit_test: The first (s1.nol_ti, s1.nol_ti4, s1.nol_limit_test, refused at f1040.line15 by s1.nol_limit). Owner decision D3 (lane x2-s1o, scout-s1-other): the sufficiency test is accepted. The premise T0 ≥ T + N holds when no deduction in scope rises with AGI. Checked against the forms (lane x2-s1o, 2026-11-15): the above-the-line deductions with phase-outs (student loan interest, IRA) fall as modified AGI rises; taxable social security benefits rise with other income, so AGI without the NOL is at least AGI with it plus N; the itemized deductions with an AGI floor (medical, casualty) and the SALT limit that falls above $500,000 of modified AGI fall as AGI rises; the larger of itemized and standard deductions keeps that direction; Schedule 1-A deductions (tips, overtime, car loan interest, seniors) phase out with modified AGI; the QBI deduction is left out of T0 and only lowers T. The one deduction that rises with AGI, charitable gifts under the AGI percentage limits, is refused when a limit binds (sa.charity_limited). NOLs from before 2018 (no 80% limit, deducted first) are refused (s1.nol_before_limit_date). The refusal sits on line 15, not on line 8a, because the test reads lines 11b and 14, which read line 8a. (i172, p. 2, "80% of the excess (if any) of taxable income computed without regard to deductions for NOLs, or Qualified Business Income (QBI), or section 250 deductions")

== Form 8949 and Schedule D — manually classified securities transactions — sales[a] ==
  (helper) Classification from records: A/B/C short-term (held 1 year or less), D/E/F long-term (held more than 1 year); G–L digital assets.

      Digital assets (such as cryptocurrency, stablecoins and non-fungible tokens):
      - G (short-term) or J (long-term): on a Form 1099-DA showing basis was reported to the IRS (box 2 checked).
      - H (short-term) or K (long-term): on a Form 1099-DA without basis, or showing basis was not reported to the IRS.
      - I (short-term) or L (long-term): not reported to you on a Form 1099-DA or a Form 1099-B.
      - A digital asset sale is never reported with box C or F.

      Inheritances and gifts:
      - Property you inherited is long-term however long you held it, unless the decedent died in 2010 and the executor elected to file Form 8939 (Revenue Procedure 2011-41 then sets the holding period).
      - If your basis in property you received as a gift is the donor's adjusted basis, your holding period began on the day the donor's did.
      - If your basis in a gift is its fair market value, the holding period starts the day after the gift.

      Counting the holding period:
      - To count the holding period, begin on the day after you acquired the property and include the day you sold it.
      - A block acquired on different dates is entered as separate transactions for its short-term and long-term parts.
      - For securities traded on an established market, the holding period begins the day after the trade date you bought them and ends on the trade date you sold them, not the settlement date.
      - U.S. Treasury notes and bonds bought at auction start the day after the Treasury gives notice of acceptance to successful bidders, and those bought by subscription at a specified yield the day after the subscription is submitted.
      - For shares bought for you by an automatic investment service, the holding period starts the day after the bank's purchase date; full shares are bought first and fractional shares last, and a share bought over more than one purchase date has a split holding period.
      - Real property bought under an unconditional contract is held from the day after you received title or took possession and assumed the burdens and privileges of ownership, whichever came first; for real property you repossessed after selling it, include the time you held it before the original sale and after the repossession, but not the time between.

      Holding periods carried over or starting on a set date:
      - Stock or securities bought in a wash sale include the holding period of those sold.
      - New stock from a nontaxable stock dividend or a spin-off, and nontaxable stock rights, include the holding period of the old stock; stock from a taxable stock dividend begins on the date of distribution, stock acquired by exercising stock rights on the date the right was exercised, and property acquired by exercising an option the day after the option was exercised.
      - Investment property acquired in a nontaxable trade, with a basis figured in whole or in part from the property traded, is held from the day after you acquired the old property.
      - Property you elected to treat as sold and reacquired on January 1, 2001 (January 2, 2001, for readily tradable stock) is treated as acquired on that date.
      - Stock received in a demutualization that qualifies as a tax-free reorganization includes the period you held the equity interest in the mutual company.

      Bad debts, money market funds, short sales, options and contracts:
      - A nonbusiness bad debt is short-term, and so is a gain or loss under the net asset value (NAV) method for a money market fund.
      - A short sale is reported in the year it closes.
      - A gain on closing a short sale is short-term if, on the date of the short sale, you held substantially identical property for 1 year or less, or if you acquired substantially identical property after the short sale but on or before the date you closed it; a loss on a short sale is long-term if, on the date of the short sale, you held substantially identical property for more than 1 year.
      - The premium you receive for writing an option that is not exercised (or repurchased) is a short-term capital gain unless you are in the business of writing options; an option you bought and did not exercise is treated as sold on the date it expired.
      - Any capital gain or loss on the sale, exchange or termination of a contract to sell property is short-term however long you held the contract, and a loss on a securities futures contract to sell is treated as a loss on closing a short sale.
      = D

Interpretation amb.2025.nft_collectible: The first (sales.special lists it, and sales.one refuses on sales.special). Computing the sale at the ordinary capital gain rates would understate the tax if such a token is a collectible, and the archive cannot settle it either way. The refusal is narrow (a token whose underlying asset is a collectible) and uses the existing gate. To be revisited once Notice 2023-27 is archived. (i1040sd, p. 11, Line 18, "Collectibles include works of art, rugs, antiques, metals (such as gold, silver, and platinum bullion), gems, stamps, coins, alcoholic beverages, and certain other tangible property."; i1040gi p. 15, Digital Assets, "digital assets include non-fungible tokens (NFTs)")
Interpretation amb.2025.sales_rounding: Preserve exact cents throughout Stage 3; whole-dollar mode remains deferred. Avoid choosing a reconciliation policy without explicit support; cents are expressly permitted. (i8949, p. 3, Rounding Off to Whole Dollars; compare i1040gi p. 23)
Interpretation amb.2025.div_gain_portions_disjoint: The first (div.bad_special tests div.special_total, boxes 2b plus 2c plus 2d, against box 2a). The issuer instructions define each box by a different kind of property: box 2b is unrecaptured section 1250 gain "from certain depreciable real property", box 2c section 1202 gain "from certain qualified small business stock", box 2d 28% rate gain "from sales or exchanges of collectibles"; and box 2a must "Include all amounts shown in boxes 2b, 2c, 2d, and 2f". No one gain comes from two of those kinds. Box 2f (section 897 gain from US real property interests) can share an amount with box 2b, and it is not in the sum. The reading can only refuse an entry, never change a computed amount, and any box 2c amount is refused in any case (div.section1202). Recorded under T-006.
 (i1099div, p. 4, Box 2a, "Include all amounts shown in boxes 2b, 2c, 2d, and 2f"; Box 2b; Box 2c; Box 2d)
Interpretation amb.2025.box2c_scope: Refuse nonzero box 2c; no exclusion or AMT amount is silently omitted. Archived Schedule D instructions require the exclusion on Form 8949 code Q and describe an AMT adjustment for 50/60/75-percent exclusions. The existing explicit exclusions are retained; the owner was notified and asked, then said continue without selecting an expansion. (i1040sd, p. 8, Gain from Form 1099-DIV and Alternative Minimum Tax)
Interpretation amb.2025.s1_nol_limit_test: The first (s1.nol_ti, s1.nol_ti4, s1.nol_limit_test, refused at f1040.line15 by s1.nol_limit). Owner decision D3 (lane x2-s1o, scout-s1-other): the sufficiency test is accepted. The premise T0 ≥ T + N holds when no deduction in scope rises with AGI. Checked against the forms (lane x2-s1o, 2026-11-15): the above-the-line deductions with phase-outs (student loan interest, IRA) fall as modified AGI rises; taxable social security benefits rise with other income, so AGI without the NOL is at least AGI with it plus N; the itemized deductions with an AGI floor (medical, casualty) and the SALT limit that falls above $500,000 of modified AGI fall as AGI rises; the larger of itemized and standard deductions keeps that direction; Schedule 1-A deductions (tips, overtime, car loan interest, seniors) phase out with modified AGI; the QBI deduction is left out of T0 and only lowers T. The one deduction that rises with AGI, charitable gifts under the AGI percentage limits, is refused when a limit binds (sa.charity_limited). NOLs from before 2018 (no 80% limit, deducted first) are refused (s1.nol_before_limit_date). The refusal sits on line 15, not on line 8a, because the test reads lines 11b and 14, which read line 8a. (i172, p. 2, "80% of the excess (if any) of taxable income computed without regard to deductions for NOLs, or Qualified Business Income (QBI), or section 250 deductions")
Interpretation amb.2025.mfs_community_property: The second, on the existing question (household.community_property), for the nine states the instructions list. The same ground as amb.2025.hoh_community_property (owner decision of 2026-10-22, integrity plan decision 10): the community income split of Pub. 555 is outside the tool. "Lived in" is broader than "domicile": a filer who lived in one of the states only temporarily is refused too, which errs toward refusing (settled as design decision D1 of the married-filing-separately scout, by the coordinator under the owner's delegation, 2026-11-15). (p504, p. 22, Community Property, "If your domicile is in a community property state during any part of your tax year, you may have community income" and "If you and your spouse file separate returns, you must report half of any income described by state law as community income")
Interpretation amb.2025.filer_under_16: The second. The refusal cites the one sentence the text has about age 16 and names the register entry; it does not claim the text forbids the return. The owner's decision of 2026-10-22 (integrity plan decision 10). A return for a child under 16 is one the tool's slice does not model well (Form 8615 is refused through scope.other_line16 only when the filer knows to answer it); the refusal makes the age an explicit entry rather than a silent assumption. (i1040gi, p. 66, Self-Select PIN, "You can’t use the Self-Select PIN method if you are a first-time filer under age 16 at the end of 2025")
Interpretation amb.2025.f4137_reported_over_received: The first (f4137.line4 subtracts with a floor of zero). Line 4 is "Include as income on Form 1040 ... line 1c", the tips not reported; a negative unreported amount has no meaning there, and column (c) is to "Include all cash and charge tips you received", so it holds at least the tips reported. The reading matters only for inconsistent entries. (f4137, p. 1, line 4, "Subtract line 3 from line 2. Include as income on Form 1040, 1040-SR, or 1040-NR, line 1c"; p. 2, Columns (c) and (d), "Include all cash and charge tips you received")
Interpretation amb.2025.mw_all_nontaxable: The first. The interview asks only about pay for care of someone living in the filer's home with them, the case Pub. 525 names; an individual care provider caring for more than five adults or ten children at home is taken to be outside the situations a filer of this tool reports. Were it wrong, line 1a or 1d would be right but Schedule 1 line 8s would exclude too much. Notice 2014-7 is not archived. (p525, p. 33, "Certain Medicaid waiver payments are treated as difficulty-of-care payments when received by an individual care provider for caring for an eligible individual (whether related or unrelated) living in the provider’s home", "Ten qualified foster individuals under age 19, or", "Five qualified foster individuals age 19 or older")
Interpretation amb.2025.alimony_screen: The first (s1.alimony, s1.alimony_unsupported; amounts under an agreement entered into after 2018, or paid after a change after 2018 that expressly applies the post-2018 rules (one that states that those rules apply, or that alimony isn't deductible by the payer or isn't included in the recipient's income), are not asked, as the questions say, and are neither income nor deductible). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, with the screens of the premise map, R-ALI-1 and R-ALI-2). The statement listing the other agreements and recipients, and the recapture worksheet, are not prepared; a payment partly child support needs the Pub. 504 allocation rules. (p504, p. 12, Alimony, "If you paid alimony to more than one person, enter the SSN or ITIN of one of the recipients"; p. 16, Recapture of Alimony; p. 13, Underpayment)
Interpretation amb.2025.s1_ebl_rental: Count them (conservative: refuses more). Form 461 is not archived to say which rental losses are business losses. (i1040se, p. 1, Reminders, "you may be subject to a business loss limitation")
Interpretation amb.2025.cod_fully_taxable: The first (s1.cod_amount, s1.cod_unsupported). Student loans are refused with the other exceptions. The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, "fully taxable canceled nonbusiness debt"). An excepted student loan discharge is nontaxable and usually comes with no Form 1099-C (p. 3, TIP), so a filer who reports one is refused rather than told how much of it to include; the conservative choice where D2 is silent. (p4681, p. 3, General Rules, "There are several exceptions and exclusions that may result in part or all of a canceled debt being nontaxable"; p. 4, Exceptions)
Interpretation amb.2025.rec_two_returns: The first (rec.two_returns, refused at rec.to_line8z). The worksheet's lines 4, 7 and 10 are figures of one return; combining two returns has no basis in the text, and the refund worksheet already refuses the like case for state refunds (amb.2025.refund_two_returns). (p525, p. 27, Worksheet 2, "Use information from your tax return for the year the expense was deducted")
Interpretation amb.2025.ewp_oid: The first (interest.penalty, totaled on s1.line18 and copied to Form 8960 line 10). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, as corrected by the verification, E4) allows an input or a screen; the instruction treats the two forms alike, so an input loses nothing. Form 1099-OID is not archived, so its box number is not named. (i1040gi, p. 95, Line 18, "The Form 1099-INT or Form 1099-OID you received will show the amount of any penalty you were charged")
Interpretation amb.2025.jury_24a_cap: The first (s1.jury_over refuses s1.line24a). The pay given to the employer is part of the pay received (p525 p. 34, "you can deduct the amount turned over to your employer"), so a larger amount is an entry error to correct, not a case to cap silently; as with w2.g_bad, the refusal fires only on an impossible entry. Premise map Q14, left open by D3. (i1040gi, p. 99, Line 24a, "Enter your jury duty pay if you gave the pay to your employer because your employer paid your salary while you served on the jury")
Interpretation amb.2025.sa_medical_negative_agi: The second (sa.agi_floor, copied to line 2). The instruction names a part of the expenses, and no part of the expenses exceeds the expenses themselves; the signed reading would deduct $324.85 of medical expenses on a return with none (engine differential case se-00255, BUG-0094). Tax-Calculator (calcfunctions.ItemDed, posagi = max(c00100, 0.)) and PolicyEngine-US both floor adjusted gross income at zero before taking the percentage. Equivalent to flooring line 3 at zero; the floor is placed on line 2's operand so that lines 3 and 4 keep their printed wording. (i1040sca, p. 1, Medical and Dental Expenses, "You can deduct only the part of your medical and dental expenses that exceeds 7.5% of the amount of your adjusted gross income on Form 1040 or 1040-SR, line 11b")
Interpretation amb.2025.multiplication_subcent: Round the product half-up to the nearest cent at the multiplication. Matches the half-up convention of the printed whole-dollar rounding rules. The whole-dollar default is unaffected: whole-dollar amounts multiplied by these rates produce exact cents, so the reading only matters when cents are turned on.
 (i1040gi, QDCGT worksheet, lines 18 and 21; Rounding Off to Whole Dollars, p. 23)
Interpretation amb.2025.sa_dep_qss: Refuse (gate sa.dep_qss, on sa.standard and sa.deduction). The worksheet is silent on the status, and taking the joint amount would be the tool's reading, not the text's. The case is rare (a widowed parent whom another person can claim). The refusal costs that filer the return; it does not compute a deduction the text does not give.
 (i1040gi, p. 35, Standard Deduction Worksheet for Dependents—Line 12e, line 3)
Interpretation amb.2025.qbi_cooperative_patron: Refuse any patron of an agricultural or horticultural cooperative, specified or not (qbi.unsupported). The owner asked for the strictly accurate reading. Refusing every patron satisfies both texts: a filer the form excludes is never computed on it, and a filer the instructions exclude is a patron the form also excludes. The cost is a refusal for a patron of a cooperative that is not specified, which the instructions would allow.
 (f8995, Form 8995 (2025), note above line 1; i8995, Who Can Use Form 8995; i1040gi line 13a)
Interpretation amb.2025.sd4952_selection: Use the detailed worksheet conditions, including line 4g and exceptions. The worksheet explicitly limits its own use and the Form 1040 line 16 instructions repeat the line 4g condition. (i1040sd, p. 15, Schedule D Tax Worksheet, applicability; compare f1040sd line 20 and i1040gi p. 36)
Scope: Form 1040 lines 1a–37 with the schedules and forms this tool implements and their refusal screens. Unsupported paths are refused with citations. No filing or transmission.
Rounding: amounts are exact cents; the return-wide whole-dollar rounding setting is deferred (see ambiguities/2025.yaml).
Estimated tax penalty: not computed; the IRS will figure it (see i1040gi, line 38).
This is a computation of published IRS forms and instructions; it is not tax or legal advice. Published instructions are not the law itself.
The user is responsible for the return. Not affiliated with or endorsed by the IRS.
